 Simon Says Move (Coflo's TSAF mix) (4:02)
 Review: The Italian collective behind the Edits Collection imprint seemingly have a bulging contacts book. Previously, they've delivered EPs of smart reworks from the likes of Rahaan, Pete Blaker, Lello Di Franco AKA LDF and the Patchouli Brothers. For their latest scalpel adventure, they've tapped up San Francisco deep house don CoFlo aka Cody Ferreira. He first turns what sounds like a percussion-rich Brazilian drum jam into a woozy deep house workout ('Polyriddim Jizm (Deeper Edit)'), before extending, thickening up and enhancing a woozier (but no less drum-heavy) workout (the organ solo-laden 'JimmySheeeeeeeeit (Extended)'. Over on side B, 'Be Good To Me' adds the vocal from Beats International's most famous cut to a jazzy, bongo-and-sub-rich broken beat rhythm, while 'Simon Says Move' is a deep hip-house gem for breakbeat heads. … Read more

 Bust A Move (4:43)
 Review: Cologne producer Funkmaster Ozone (real name Johann Wagner) first caught the P-funk and electrofunk bug in the 1980s, though it took another 20 years for him to start releasing his distinctive take on the synth-heavy style. Here he returns to Spanish outlet The Sleeperz Records with his third seven-inch of the decade. A-side 'Stankface' has a real Roger Troutman/Zapp feel - all effects-laden talkbox vocals, pitch-bend synth solos, squelchy synth-bass, unfussy drum machine beats and tons of far-sighted musical colour. 'Bust a Move' explores similar sonic territory (complete with even more extensive talkbox vocals), albeit with a punchier and more break-dance ready electro beat. … Read more

 Untitled 002
 Review: An unnamed artist reworks two cult classics from, so say the notes, Scandinavia's dark synth-pop pioneers here and does it by keeping the cold, vintage atmosphere intact while updating the production. Both tracks are untitled, and the listing gives no hint of which songs are being reimagined, so part of the fun is guessing. The first pairs sleek electronic grooves with an almost Caribbean-style steel drum melody up top and nagging, deadpan vocals. The flip has more jump and boucle to the analogue drums with fat bass and playful energy. … Read more

 Etre Heureux (feat Cham - Anoraak remix)

 Etre Heureux (feat Cham)
 Review: Dennis Liber is the production alias of Dionisios Tsinaslanidis, a Greek-Turkish DJ and producer who's currently based in Tulum, Mexico, and whose uncle is veteran Swiss producer Jamie Lewis, of Purple Music fame. This four-track EP for Razor-N-Tape serves as a showcase for his trademark sound, which is rooted in house and disco but also leans heavily on lounge, Balearic and tropical influences, with all four tracks featuring lilting female vocals courtesy of Yasmin Kadernian and Charm. Argentinian producer Shoao (real name Lautaro Gonzalez, and a fellow resident of Tulum) also gets involved on 'Femme Papillon', while French producer Anoraak (Frederic Riviere) gives 'Etre Heureux', which rides a lolloping Latin rhythm in its Original form, a slightly housier makeover. One to check if your floor's more about the sway than the stomp.. … Read more

 Amante Bailar (Magari Wrong Speed dub) (4:03)
 Review: PBR Streetgang continue to offer-up heavyweight - and frequently radically reworked - re-edits via their hush-hush Lost Property series. The long-serving duo serve up a pair of collaborative edits across the EP, with the Bjork-goes-organ-rich deep house brilliance of 'There's More 2 Lyfe (PBR Refix)' (A1) being joined by a deliciously heavy and squelchy dancefloor dub of an early 80s electrofunk favourite once mixed by Francois Kevorkian. The other two tweaks come from Magari, a solo alias of one half of the duo. He delivers an 'aerobic dub' of Italo-meets-Hi-NRG throbber 'Excite Her' and a wonderfully pitched-down, wrong-speed rework of Italo number 'Amante Bailar' that's worth the admission price on its own. … Read more

 They Don't Love You (2:58)
 Review: More 'expensive sounding' re-edits from Los Angeles nu-disco don Luxxury, a producer who has been offering up periodic EPs of floor-focused reworks since 2014. This time round, he begins by cutting up and chugging out the biggest selling 12" single of all time, retaining some familiar elements while turning it into a synth-powered house loop jam, before giving a warming, electric piano-heavy disco-sing-along the same treatment on 'A Woman's Man'. Side B sports a trio of revisions, with the dubbed-out (and genuinely brilliant) 'Crystal Visions' (seemingly a multi-track edit of a Fleetwood Mac fave) being followed by the stomping, funk-goes-house heaviness of 'I'm a Man' and the end-of-night delight that is classic rock re-rub 'They Don't Love You'. … Read more

 Stay With Me (4:17)
 Review: The Sleeperz Records founder Ivan Makvel (real name Ivan De Gese) has spent much of his career working with other artists. He continues in that vein on this single, which sees him lend his voice and arrangement skills to two tracks from Argentine synth-funk and boogie revivalists Midas Touch. The trio set their stall out with the jaunty and ear-catching 'Across The Room', where the Sleeperz main man's distinctively soulful and dreamy lead vocals rise above Dam Funk grooves, P-funk synth-splashes and nods to the dancefloor colour of NYC boogie legends D-Train. On 'Stay With Me', De Gese takes to the talkbox over a wonderfully squelchy and kaleidoscopic deep P-funk jam. … Read more

 Disco 86 - "Tu Amor" (6:09)
 Review: He's back once again is Monsieur Van Pratt with more of his so-wrong-they're-right disco edits. This fight volume has two edits reshaping source material with the usual deft touch. His opener 'Linda' runs on clipped drums, tight bass loops and bright string stabs then 'Oye Un Momento' keeps its original vocal but reinforces the groove with heavier kicks and 'El Ritmo Que Te Atrap' has a kitsch Europe feel. On the flip, Disco 86 shifts towards something classier with 'Crees Que Soy Sexy' riding lush chords and plenty of glistening melodies drawn from the Rod Stewart original and 'Tu Amor' is packed with electric vocals and retro future keys for a full send finish. … Read more

 Dimmi
 Review: A couple of years ago, Italian singer/songwriter Beatrice De Cristofaro made the move from her native Southern Italy to Napoli, a city renowned for its colourful and hard-to-pigeonhole musical output. She fell in with some of the city's leading musicians and producers, Whodamanny included, and recorded this fine debut album as Only Bee. Featuring a fine array of contributing musicians and producers, the eponymous album offers a nostalgic dip into Italian house music's turn-of-the-90s roots, with tactile vocal deep house and kaleidoscopic early 90s style Italo-house being joined by nostalgic downtempo head-nodders, acid-style jackers and revivalist street soul. It sounds like a long lost album from 1990 or '91, but that's no bad thing. Certainly, it's a hugely entertaining debut. … Read more

 21st Floor Mambo (4:30)
 Review: Originally pressed on the obscure UK independent Paladin in the early 1980s, 'Foot In The Door' has been lately circulating on compilations after getting bootlegged and commanding serious money on the collector's market, all of which helped add to its mythology. Now remastered with extra percussive punch and pressed on a limited 10" alongside the ass-wiggling 'Samba Doo Bonnay' and bright sax stabs of '21st Floor Mambo,' it finally becomes widely available. The Airto-styled Batucada percussion and samba-boogie groove place it firmly at the intersection of Brit-funk and what would soon be called acid jazz. A lost classic. … Read more

 Penny Delight (Radic The Myth vocal dub)
 Review: Parisian producer Kelton Prima spent the formative part of his career delivering lo-fi and off-kilter outings on Jamal Moss's Mathematics Recordings imprint, but in recent years has moved closer towards polished and synth-heavy fusions of house and nu-disco. He leans into the latter on this first Ravenelli Disco Club outing. A-side opener 'Noisette Chocolate' is particularly smooth and sensual, with Prima combining warming garage-house grooves and organ licks with glassy-eyed 80s soul samples and vibrant synth-sounds. German house legend Ian Pooley remixes, delivering a version that sits somewhere between classic garage-house and his own disco-fied deep house jams of the 90s. Side B is all about 'Penny Delight', with the artist's sun-splashed and gently breezy deep house original being accompanied by a more peak-time ready 'Rock The Myth Vocal Dub'. … Read more

 Asha Puthli & Say She She - "Pawa!" (Dino Soccio extended Disco mix) (6:34)
 Review: Ragz and Turbitto's Naya Beat label has thus far excelled at blending old and new South Asian music with 21st century house, disco and Balearica. they once more invite admired producers and artist friends to rework tracks from the label's growing catalogue. The key cut here - at least in terms of sheer dancefloor potential - is Woolfy v Projections' fine revision of Ragz & Turbitto's 'Nanan Da Oyar De Gaya'. Sitting somewhere between psychedelic Indian electrofunk and Tom Tom Club's 'Wordyrappinghood', it's a stellar, funky and sub-heavy rub. Elsewhere, Turbitto and Ragz give hyped outfit Boys Shorts the Naya Beat makeover, Psychemagik man Thomas Banks turns Pinky Ann Rihal's 'Tru Asia' into a dark wave throbber and Dino Soccio provides a fine disco extension of Asha Pushli and Say She She's 'Pawa'. … Read more

 Gino Tonico & Tino Arancino - "Fuoco" (Spargifiamma mix)
 Review: Here's something pleasingly expansive: a five-track multi-artist missive in which a sequence of artists give their interpretations of the same core cut, 'Fuoco'. Devionaze handles mix duties on Gino Tonico and Tino Arcanio's EP-opening version, an acid-flecked bunch of Italo-disco-meets-disco-house chunkiness rich in glassy-eyed male vocals, Chic-style guitar licks and heavy bass guitar, before Dottor Ducato (an artist better-known as IFS) drops an all-action take that draws inspiration from the more muscular and synth-driven end of the Italo-disco spectrum. Castariva's take is a jaunty, tops-off delight that emphasises glistening guitars and Bobby Orlando style bass and Scasso's version is a hypnotic and trippy throb-job. To round things oiff, Tonico and Arcanio return with a heavier and tougher take. … Read more

 Punk Party
 Review: It was way back in 1979 when Trinidadian steel pan player Lennox "Boogsie" Sharpe gathered a crack collection of musicians and recorded his now cult debut album, Phase II. Renowned as "the Mozart of steel pan", the album showcased his then unique fusion of Caribbean rhythms, disco and jazz-funk, with a stunning cover of Roy Ayers favourite 'Can't You See Me' being joined by a wealth of compositions from the man himself. As you'd expect, his thrilling steel pan playing regularly takes centre stage, but the quality of the accompanying musicians and Sharpe's fabulous arrangements mean it's far more than a steel band album. Astonishingly, this Mr Bongo edition is the first official reissue of the cult classic, making it a genuinely essential purchase. … Read more

 Review: American funk freighters Fatback Band (here credited simply as Fatback) are the little-known pioneers of dance music as we know it, their hit song 'Oops Up Side Your Head' known for popularising four-to-the-floor beats, paving the way for house and techno as we know it. This 1980 album, Hot Box, is one of their lesser-known favourites, includes such brilliants as 'Backstrokin' and 'King Tim III' , the latter of which is often cited as one of the earliest commercially released rap recordings.  Dino Solera - "Classically Elise"
 Review: Slacker biographies will locate the beginning and end of Giorgio Moroder's career at 'I Feel Love' and leave it at that. But Repertoire's new triple CD compilation corrects that perception, locating him properly inside Musicland Studios in Munich over a ten-year period, in which time and place he built his reputation as a producer with a throng of collaborators, including Munich Machine, Joe Esposito and Roberta Kelly. Among the bangers here, beside his Donna Summer go-tos, are 'Beat The Clock' - his cracking production for surrealist pop duo Sparks - and the Flashdance soundtrack cut 'Lady Lady', one of the first to prove electro-disco as a viable bridge language for Hollywood soundtracking. … Read more

 J Dalton - "Game" (4:38)
 Review: For the uninitiated, Rotation Sound System is a collective of veteran crate diggers, DJs, producers and party promoters spearheaded by Mind Fair's Dean Meredith and Ben Shenton, and Wreckin' Havoc trio Rob J, Stuart Robinson and Rich Hall. For years they've thrown an annual garden party (using their custom soundsystem of the same name) and now Leng has offered them a chance to curate their own comp. As you'd expect, the 12-track collection is piled high with largely unknown gems, from the tactile, syntth-heavy Balearic breeze of Moondata's 'Let The Sunshine In (Dub)' and Roger Renning's chugging dub disco aligned 'Cecelia', to the oddball boogie-pop of 'Music' by Amy Cooper, the Italo-disco inspired throb of Michael Heart's 'Some Girls (Instrumental)' and the loved-up synth-pop-goes-proto-house of Jean Louis Murat's 'To Garder Pres De Moi (Dream Remix)'. … Read more

 Coma II (4:46)
 Review: In 1989, a year or so after gatecrashing the charts with sample-heavy debut single 'Theme From S'Express', Mark Moore's early UK house outfit S'Express dropped their debut album, Original Soundtrack. Here it returns to vinyl for the first time since. Opening with a tweaked take on their early UK acid house gem, it's undoubtedly a time capsule - all drum machine-driven Chicago house rhythms, glassy-eyed vocals, spacey synth hooks, wide-eyed female vocals and all manner of ear-catching samples lifted from everything from Star Trek records, classic disco jams and Italo-disco oddities, to fiery funk jams, boogie favourites and crackly soul sevens. It's not all house of course - there are nods to street soul and synth-pop - but it remains hugely reflective of the period in which it was made and released. … Read more

 Final Signal (Domenico Torti remix)
 Review: Wisely, French label Because Music has decided to doff a cap to one of its country's most significant disco-era exports, Didier Marouani's astronaut suit-wearing cosmic disco pioneers, Space. It's a smart move, as Marouani and his cohorts created a throbbing, synth-heavy sound that would go on to influence the later development of Italo-disco. CD1 looks back at the band's glory years (1977-78) via a mix of celebrated singles ('Magic Fly', 'Tango In Space', 'Carry On, Turn Me On'), lesser-celebrated gems and album tracks. CD2, meanwhile, celebrates the band's legacy via recent remixes from the likes of Dimitri From Paris, Toxic Avenger, The Reflex, Alex Gopher and, more surprisingly, Chicago house great Joe Smooth and Mr Root. … Read more

 Baby's Paradise (5:20)
 Review: It was way back in 1977 when Monaco-raised composer-producer Didier Marouani launched what would become his most celebrated and commercially successful outfit: pioneering cosmic disco band Space. In the space of 24 months, he and his accomplices delivered a string of intergalactic, synth-powered albums and singles that not only laid the foundations for he later Italo-disco wave, but also effectively defined the 'space disco' sound. This 'best of' retrospective does a good job in telling the story of the band through a mixture of colossal underground club hits ('Magic Fly', 'Carry On Turn Me On', 'Tango In Space') and lesser-celebrated workouts. It should be essential listening for disco heads old and new. … Read more

 Spring (reprise) (3:42)
 Review: In 1976, on the back of the success of the Giorgio Moroder-produced Love To Love You baby full-length, disco diva Donna Summer decided to record her first 'concept album' - an idea familiar to rock fans but then a rarity within dancefloor culture. Once more recorded with Moroder and his 'Munich Machine' house band, Four Seasons of Love was Summer's attempt to chronicle the changing seasons in disco form. Here reissued for National Album Day 2026, it remains a classic of its type - a collection of extended disco workouts of the type that made Casablanca Records one of the most commercially successful labels of the 1970s. Beginning with the swelling 'Spring Affair' and closing with a solo-laden 'reprise' of the same cut, it remains a lesson in the artistic potential of disco. … Read more

 Add to playlist
 The Casablanca Studio Albums (10xLP)
 Review: The graceful style of disco queen Donna Summer is memorialised in a set of ten coloured vinyl LPs - light blue, hot pink, green, yellow, orange, indigo and violet - housing the seven albums she made between 1975 and 1979 with Giorgio Moroder and Pete Bellotte. Legendarily, two years before the precedent for extended remixes was set by 'I Feel Love', Neil Bogart heard the original four-minute 'Love To Love You Baby' at a dinner party and asked Moroder for 17 minutes; Summer recorded the extended version lying on the studio floor. Two years later, Brian Eno heard 'I Feel Love' playing in the Berlin studio where he and Bowie were working and declared it the sound of the future. The box runs from that moment through Bad Girls, just before Summer's conversion to evangelical Christianity closed the chapter. … Read more

 Her Way (3:35)
 Review: Long before Chicago house became a global force, Jesse Saunders and Vince Lawrence were busy blurring the lines between disco, electro-funk and synth-pop. Originally released in 1984, their Dance Party Album is a formative work with Italo-inspired futurism, Midwest funk and raw, party-ready energy running throughout. From the infectious 'I Am The DJ' to the sleek synths of 'Fantasy' and punk-funk snap of 'Fast Cars', it's a vital document of house music's earliest years. Numero's expanded edition adds two fresh takes on 'I Am The DJ', so this historic party-starter has an extra kick. … Read more

 Ken Goes To Quadrant Park
 Review: Naturally, we have no idea who is behind the Bar:low edit series, though they do have eclectic tastes. Previous missives from the shadowy scalpel fiend have included reworks of cuts from Cerrone, Bruce Springsteen and Nine Inch Nails. There’s a bit more mystery about this one, with opener ‘Ken Goes Kosmic’ delivering a fine extension and rearrangement of a chugging, ethereal and synth-heavy slab of pitched-down 80s cosmic disco. ‘The Raver Returns’ sounds like a pass on a mid-to-late-80s alternative synth-pop workout wrapped in echoing guitar licks and psychedelic acid lines, while ‘Ken Goes To Quadrant Park’ re-boots a familiar, wide-eyed anthem from the rave era. We assume their hero Ken Barlow would approve. … Read more

 Review: Munich staple Permanent Vacation's 20th anniversary EP series continues. As with its predecessors, 'XX EP 3' is packed with previously unheard cuts from imprint artists and invited guests.  Whale Love Is
 Review: Astonishingly, four years have now passed since the second missive from mysterious scalpel fiends Cheeky Chops, a naturally publicity shy artist known not only for excellent, multi-track rearrangements of suitably big records, but also a love of punning alternative titles. We got both of these elements in spades on this delayed volume three. Side A sports an excellent extension of a 1978 soaring disco classic from Cheryl Lynn (here amusingly titled 'You Shaved My Way'), while on the flip you'll find a luxuriously soulful disco version of 'That's The Way Love Is' ('Whale Love Is'), a song later made famous by Chicago house act Ten City. There's no needless additional beats or 21st century instrumentation, just careful rearrangements of classic cuts. More please! … Read more

 [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1186357-01-02-03.mp3] [audio: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1186357-01-02-03.mp3] 
 Crosby Street (Loft Acetate - #4)
 Review: The Inner City Sound Archives series was founded on the premise of showcasing 'unreleased NYC loft cuts' from the 70s and 80s - basically alternate takes, demos and unheard mixes rescued from reel-to-reel tapes. Volume three boasts six more slabs of previously buried treasure, starting with the horn-toting, loved-up instrumental disco warmth of 'Midnight Passage (Tape Transfer - 81c)'. 'Afterglow (Garage Reel #7)' is a more elastic, synth-splashed chunk of body-moving jazz-funk/disco fusion from the early boogie era, while 'Mercury Rising (Rough Mix)' is a rushing, hands-aloft chunk of orchestrated disco magic. Turn to side B for the breezy 'West Side Motion (NYC Edit 80)' - all jazzy guitar licks, warming grooves and life-affirming chords - as well as the classy 'Golden Hours (unreleased rehearsal)' and the 'Loft Acetate -#4' version of slow-build, jazz-flecked dub reggae gem 'Crosby Street'. … Read more
 Played by: [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/chart/35990320] Superbreak , [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/chart/35968188] PBR STREETGANG
